- R-1008 — Medium, FIVE CONSECUTIVE CLOSURES RAN THE SELF-USE TRACK AND NOT ONE LANDED A REPAIR,
BECAUSE THE GENERATOR HANDED THE LOCAL MODEL FINDINGS THAT NAME NO REPAIR AND THE RUNNER RESOLVED THE
PRODUCT-DEFAULT ROLES FOR WORK THAT REPAIRS THIS REPOSITORY. Raised under operator amendment
amend0920-selfuse-real, Part B.5, at `43d14817`, after searching the open set and its archive for
`self-use`, `self_use`, `SU-0`, `blocked` and `hollow` under §3 item 30: R-0784 and R-0999 each
register ONE such run's blocked strings because closure precondition 6 requires it, and R-0838
registers the generator re-selecting one finding — none describes the TRACK failing to land anything
across five closures, which is this id's subject and is why it is minted rather than added to those.
THE MEASUREMENT, read from the ledger and from `.agent/selfuse_f269` through `.agent/selfuse_f273`:
SU-019 (F268), SU-020 (F269), SU-021 (F270), SU-022 (F271) and SU-023 (F273) each planned, each ran,
each ended blocked or budget-stopped at the approval gate, and no self-use run in the track's history
has ever produced a landed repair. TWO CAUSES, both measured rather than supposed. FIRST, the item.
SU-023's finding was R-0499, whose own paragraph says the sweep goes red "about once in twenty runs"
and that "THE FAILING NODE ID HAS NEVER BEEN CAPTURED", and which names no `FIX:` at all — so the job
asked a builder to repair a defect that does not reproduce on demand and whose evidence does not
exist. R-0999 records what followed in its own words: the builder changed no file, and the reviewer
failed it over the empty diff. SECOND, the runner. `run_next_self_use_item` resolved `builder` and
`reviewer` through `resolve_role_config`, whose product default is `ollama` on
`muse-glimmer:latest` — the model every one of the five runs used, per the Gate: F273 R23 entry — for
work that repairs THIS repository against THIS repository's review discipline, which is the hardest
work the product does. WHY MEDIUM AND NOT LOW: nothing on disk is wrong and every run stopped at its
gate as a blocked job must, so no product state is corrupt; what is lost is a closure precondition
that has proven nothing for five features running, which makes "Remedy is used on Remedy" a ceremony
rather than a check — and a precondition that cannot fail is the class this record already treats as
a defect. FIX, AND IT IS BUILT BY THIS AMENDMENT RATHER THAN OWED: Tier 1 offers only a finding whose
paragraph names a repair and does not say that repair is held by something the run does not control,
and both sides of the run resolve from the new `self_use` role — the configured frontier provider, on
this path's own budget of 8 calls and 1.00 USD (DECISION amend0920-selfuse-real D2). THE PROOF IS THE
NEXT CLOSURE'S OWN RUN, and this finding resolves when one lands a repair: a `Done: R-1008` line is
owed by the first closure whose self-use run produces a diff its reviewer passes, and by no earlier
one. Until then it stays open however green the new tests are, because tests prove the wiring and
only a run proves the track. Owner: F282.

- R-1009 — Medium, MAIN'S HOSTED CI HAS BEEN RED ON BOTH COLUMNS SINCE `43d14817` BECAUSE A CLOSURE
ADDED AN ASSERTION THAT THE HOSTED ENVIRONMENT CANNOT MEET: IT REQUIRES A NETWORK PROBE THAT THE
`ollama` PACKAGE MAKES, AND CI INSTALLS ONLY `.[dev]`, WHICH DOES NOT CARRY IT. Raised under operator
amendment amend0920-selfuse-real at `aeadfa45`, from that amendment's own pull request 261 reading
red, after searching the open set and its archive for `test_study_run_dispatch_e2e`, `R-0978`,
`R-0961`, `study run` and `3.12` under §3 item 30: R-0984 is the 3.12 column's OTHER defect (a
str-mixed enum's rendering) and is a different mechanism on the same column; R-0978 and R-0961
concern this same test's host reachability and its subprocess timeout and are both RESOLVED in the
archive; no finding describes this one. THE DEFECT, measured four ways and not inferred. (1) THE
FAILING NODE is `tests/cli/test_study_cmd.py::TestStudyCommandReachability::
test_study_run_dispatch_e2e`, failing `AssertionError: study run never asked the model host the test
gave it` / `assert []`. (2) IT REPRODUCES AT THE FORK POINT, ON BOTH COLUMNS, BEFORE ANY COMMIT OF
THIS AMENDMENT: run 35473013938 on `main` at `43d14817` — the very commit this branch was cut from —
fails that same node on `ci (3.10)` and on `ci (3.12)`, so under amend0917-throughput rule 2 it is
not this amendment's to have broken. (3) THE CAUSE IS THE ABSENT PACKAGE, read from the hosted job's
own install line: job 106056424649 prints `Successfully installed annotated-types … typing-inspection`
— twenty-two distributions, and `ollama` is not among them, because `.github/workflows/ci.yml` ran
`python3 -m pip install -e ".[dev]"` and `pyproject.toml`'s `dev` extra is pytest, pytest-xdist,
ruff, mypy, pytest-cov and coverage. `make_structured_call_fn` in
`packages/orchestration/intake.py` answers `None` at `import ollama` BEFORE it constructs a client
or contacts any host, which a direct probe confirms: with `sys.modules["ollama"]` broken the factory
returns `None` and the loopback listener is never accepted. So the assertion is unmeetable in that
environment, whatever the product does. (4) THE SAME NODE PASSES WHERE THE PACKAGE IS PRESENT: `1
passed in 84.45s` in the primary checkout at `aeadfa45`, which has `ollama` installed. WHEN IT
ENTERED: `80f7c529`'s run 35412813460 was green; the `assert asked` line was added between there and
`43d14817` by F273's closure, whose own hosted CI runs only AFTER its last commit — which is the
trap R-0984's carry-forward reason already names, sprung a second time on a different node. WHY
MEDIUM: nothing on disk is wrong and no product behaviour is at fault — the cost is that `main` is
unmergeable-by-the-rules for every feature after F273 until it is fixed, and that a reader of the red
column is told a reachability claim failed when the environment simply cannot test it. FIX, APPLIED
BY THIS AMENDMENT because it is what blocked its own merge and AGENTS.md's amend0820-gate-autonomy
makes a red check on the open pull request's branch the session's work order: `.github/workflows/
ci.yml` installs `.[dev,ollama]`, which brings the hosted environment into line with the one the
suite is verified in, keeps the assertion at FULL STRENGTH rather than skipping it, starts no server,
and changes nothing in process — `tests/conftest.py`'s `_no_live_ollama_reach` refuses
`ollama.Client` for every in-process test without the `real_ollama` marker, and its own comment says
the not-installed path exists to reproduce the hosted behaviour, so installed and absent are already
equivalent there; only a SUBPROCESS test can reach a host, and only the loopback one its own fixture
opens. RED PROOF, and it is the only honest one for an environment defect, the same shape R-0984's
own FIX clause names: the hosted column is RED at `43d14817` and at `aeadfa45` and GREEN at the tip
of this branch. MEASURED: run 35503727182, the first run carrying the extra, lists this node in
NEITHER column's failures — `gh run view 35503727182 --log-failed | grep -c
test_study_run_dispatch_e2e` reads 0 against 2 FAILED lines in total, both of them a DIFFERENT node
(`tests/orchestration/test_ci_workflow.py::test_hosted_workflow_selects_no_tests_of_its_own`, which
the repair's own comment tripped by spelling a stage marker expression inside the workflow file and
which is repaired in the commit after it, by rewording the comment and never by touching the guard).
So the node this finding is about went red-to-green on the hosted runner, which is the one
environment where it could be shown at all. Owner: F282.

- R-1004 — Medium, THE DATA-ROOT GUARD WALKS THE WHOLE DATA ROOT TWICE PER PYTEST SESSION, SO EVERY RUN IN THIS CHECKOUT PAYS FOR THE OPERATOR'S DISK. `tests/conftest.py::_data_root_fingerprint` builds a sorted list of `(relative path, kind, size, mtime_ns)` for EVERY entry below the configured data root by `os.walk` plus one `lstat` each, and `pytest_configure` and `pytest_sessionfinish` each call it once, to arm and to check R-0803's pollution guard. Measured on 2026-09-20 on the operator's root, which `data usage --json` reads at 8056050 entries: `--collect-only` on a single new test file read `18 tests collected in 83.22s`, against `18 passed in 0.32s` for the same file in a worktree where the repo-local `.data` does not exist and the fingerprint returns None at once; the same 19-node reading of `test_job_apply.py` took 139.14s in the primary checkout and 10.82s in a worktree. The cost is therefore about 128 seconds per session on this machine plus a list of eight million tuples held in memory, it scales with the operator's disk rather than with the suite, and it is paid by every round's gates and by the closure suite. The guard itself is right and F273 built it for a real defect; only its cost is wrong. FIX, for whoever takes it: fingerprint what the guard actually needs — the top-level children and their own `lstat`, or a bounded hash rather than a retained list — or arm the full walk only when a cheap reading already differs, and state in the guard's docstring what it can and cannot see afterwards. Severity Medium: nothing is wrong on disk and no colour changes; the cost is real and repeated. Registered at F276 R5 from the reviewer's own measurement. Owner: F282 — Findings paydown v2.

- R-1005 — Medium, A DEADLINE BUDGET STOP CANNOT FINALIZE ITS RUN MANIFEST, SO THE ONE LIMIT WHOSE EXHAUSTION IS CERTAIN LEAVES ITS JOB NON-TERMINAL. Found by a research helper while building T004 and reported with its reproduction: a job given `budgets={"deadline": <a past time>}` and no other limit stops at its first safe point with `stop_reason='budget_exhausted:deadline'`, and the manifest write then fails with `ManifestError: the bound record does not survive a strict decode round-trip`, leaving the job `planned` rather than STOPPED, with and without microseconds in the timestamp. It is the class R-0225 already records for `max_cost_usd` and that F276 R6's own T004 repair removes for FIELD NAMES by deriving `run_manifest._BUDGET_ALLOWED_KEYS` from `JobBudgets.model_fields`; this one is a VALUE that does not survive the round trip, which that derivation does not reach. Severity Medium: the deadline limit is documented and configurable, and a job that hits it is left in a state no terminal reader will report. FIX: make the manifest's budget round-trip accept the `datetime` `JobBudgets` declares, with a test that stops a job on a deadline and reads its persisted state as stopped. Registered at F276 R5. Owner: F282 — Findings paydown v2.

Landed: R-1006 — the `remedy job budget` limits listing now names `min_free_disk_bytes`, guarded over `JobBudgets.model_fields`; landed at this round's C2.

- R-1007 — Medium, THE SELF-USE RUNNER'S DEFAULT PROVIDER-CALL BUDGET IS EXACTLY THE NUMBER OF CALLS ITS OWN DEFAULT ROUND COUNT SPENDS, SO A TASK THAT USES EVERY REPAIR ROUND IS RECORDED AS A BUDGET STOP AND THE REVIEWER'S VERDICT IT ACTUALLY REACHED IS LOST. Raised by the reviewer at F276 R8's gate, from the run that closure precondition 6 itself consumed, and MEASURED at `8da83220` against the persisted record rather than against the handback. `self_use_runner.run_next_self_use_item` declares `max_provider_calls: int | None = 6`. The job it started resolved `max_rounds=3` and `repair_rounds_allowed=2`, both from `'default'`, and a round spends two provider calls, the builder's and the reviewer's — which is not assumed here but divided out of the two recorded numbers, `budget_actuals.provider_call_count = 6` over the three rounds `repair_rounds_used = 2` of `repair_rounds_allowed = 2` records. So the ceiling and the loop's own maximum are the SAME NUMBER, and the ceiling is reached at the last reviewer call of the last permitted repair round. The persisted job reads `status = "stopped"` with `stop.reason = "budget_exhausted:max_provider_calls"` and `stop.source = "budget"`, while its one task T001 reads `status = "pending"`, `final_status = "stopped"`, `task_attempt_state = "active"`, `tripped_limit = ""` and, decisively, `reviewer_verdict = ""`. THE PRODUCT EFFECT IS THE EMPTY FIELD. Earlier self-use runs that reached repair exhaustion recorded `final_status=repair_exhausted; reviewer_verdict=fail`, and that is the reading which told those reviewers the model had genuinely failed the task rather than that the run had been cut short; counted mechanically over `.agent/live_review.md` and its archive at `8da83220`, that exact string appears 27 times against 12 DISTINCT job ids, so it is the settled shape of this outcome and not an anecdote. This closure's run cannot be read that way at all: `describe_self_use_run_defects` answers `job c1dba9c3d7874968 (stopped): stop_reason=budget_exhausted:max_provider_calls; stop_source=budget` and `T001 (pending): final_status=stopped`, which name a budget and say nothing about what the reviewer decided. A precondition that exists to surface Remedy's own defects through a real run has thereby lost the run's own outcome. TWO STRINGS, ONE ID, per §3 item 30 and per the precedent the resolved `R-0784` set in its own text: they are the job-level and the task-level view of one event. SEARCHED BEFORE MINTING, also per item 30: `max_provider_calls` occurs zero times in `.agent/live_review.md`; `R-0784`, which held this family open across successive closures and carries three `Recurrence:` paragraphs in the live ledger, is RESOLVED and archived, its `Done:` paragraph naming `8a162c39` at F273 R7 C3; and the open finding nearest it, `R-0999`, describes a run that ended BLOCKED after ONE round with BOTH repair rounds UNSPENT, which is the opposite reading of the same machinery. Severity Medium and not High: the job is correctly terminal, nothing on disk is corrupt, and no operator decision is made on the lost field — what is lost is a diagnosis. FIX: derive that default from the round configuration the runner actually starts the job under instead of hard-coding it, or at minimum make it strictly greater than two times one plus `repair_rounds_allowed`, so the budget is a backstop rather than a tie with the loop; with a test that drives a job to repair exhaustion under the DEFAULT budget with a fake provider and asserts the recorded outcome names the reviewer's verdict and not `budget_exhausted`. Registered at F276 R9. Owner: F282 — Findings paydown v2.

- R-1015 — Medium, THE SELF-USE GENERATOR COPIES A LEDGER PARAGRAPH VERBATIM INTO A TRACKED FILE AND SCREENS IT FOR EVERYTHING EXCEPT THE REPOSITORY'S OWN RETIRED VOCABULARY, SO GENERATING A CLOSURE'S SELF-USE ITEM CAN TURN `tests/docs/` RED — AND DID. Registered by the planner and reviewer of F277's seventh session at the round 13 gate, measured at `47116516`, after searching the open set and its archive under §3 item 30 for `self_use_generator`, `retired`, `promote`, `KEPT_BY_SENSE` and `self_use_queue.json`: R-0838 registers the generator RE-SELECTING a finding an entry already targets and R-1008 registers the TRACK never landing a repair, and neither describes what the generator WRITES, which is this id's subject and is why it is minted rather than added to those. THE MEASUREMENT. `packages/orchestration/self_use_generator.py::_ledger_tier` builds an item's `why` and `job_markdown` from the chosen finding's ledger paragraph verbatim, and `append_generated_item` writes both into `scripts/self_use_queue.json`, which is tracked. `tests/docs/test_retired_promote_word.py` reads every file `git ls-files` lists under `apps/`, `packages/`, `scripts/`, `tests/`, `docs/` and `README.md` — `docs/roadmap/` and the guard itself excepted — and fails on any `promot*` token outside `KEPT_BY_SENSE`. F277's closure generated `SU-025` from `R-0820`, whose paragraph contains the phrase "a promotion result" while listing the types `.status` is polymorphic across, so the token `promotion` entered a tracked file and `test_no_file_outside_the_map_carries_the_word` went red at `1 failed, 313 passed` against `tests/docs/` alone. The branch tip was red from `47116516` until this round repaired it. THE DEFECT IS THAT THE SCREEN EXISTS AND IS ONE CONDITION SHORT, which is why this is Medium rather than Low: `_ledger_tier` already refuses a paragraph carrying a line shaped like a markdown heading or an `Acceptance:` marker, with a comment reading "a ledger paragraph is prose, not markdown authored for this purpose, so it is NEVER trusted to be safe to embed" — the distrust is already the design, and the one hazard it does not check is the vocabulary guard that every other tracked file in this repository is held to. Any future closure whose oldest open Low or Medium finding happens to quote a retired word reproduces this exactly, and the closure is where it hurts most, because a red branch is a closure blocker. WHAT LANDED THIS ROUND IS A STOPGAP AND SAYS SO ON ITS FACE: a `KEPT_BY_SENSE` entry for `scripts/self_use_queue.json` under the guard's existing sense `H: history or a retired-word guard`, carrying the single token `promotion`, because a verbatim quotation of an append-only record is a QUOTATION and not a USE — the R-0584 distinction §3 item 20 already draws, that a token a finding QUOTES is not a token it USES. It weakens nothing, and the reviewer proved that rather than asserting it: with the entry in place a SECOND retired token planted in the queue file is still caught, and a token listed in the entry that does not occur in the file is still rejected, both measured at exit 1 in a disposable worktree at `24f36eaf`. What the stopgap costs is maintenance nobody will remember — the entry tracks the CURRENT pending item and goes stale the moment the queue turns over. FIX: `_ledger_tier` screens the chosen paragraph against the retired-vocabulary guard the same way it screens for headings, and on a hit SKIPS that finding and takes the next eligible one rather than raising, because raising would block a closure over a word; when the screen lands, this `KEPT_BY_SENSE` entry is deleted in the same commit and the guard's own docstring rule — remove the entry once it lists no token — is what makes that visible. The repair is in the generator, which is F258's code and not F277's scope. Owner: F282.

- R-1016 — Medium, THE CLOSURE'S SELF-USE RUN ENDED BLOCKED WITH BOTH REPAIR ROUNDS UNSPENT AND AN EMPTY RESULT DIFF, BECAUSE THE CONFIGURED `claude-cli` PROVIDER ANSWERED NOTHING ON EITHER SIDE; BOTH STRINGS `describe_self_use_run_defects` RETURNED ARE REGISTERED HERE BECAUSE CLOSURE PRECONDITION 6 REQUIRES IT. The two strings, verbatim and in order, are `job 86f628f5e4fb4e0c (blocked): task_T001_gate_failed: final_status=provider_unavailable; missing_reviewer_output` and `T001 (blocked): completion_gate_failed: final_status=provider_unavailable; missing_reviewer_output`. Registered by the planner and reviewer of F277's seventh session at the round 13 gate, from round 13's self-use run (`SU-025`, job `86f628f5e4fb4e0c`, task run `9e1544e204ea4091`, builder and reviewer both `claude-cli` `claude-sonnet-4-6`), after searching the ledger and its archive under §3 item 30 for `provider_unavailable`, `missing_reviewer_output` and `claude-cli`: R-0784 and R-0999 each register one earlier closure's blocked strings the same way and neither names this final status — R-0999's is `review_inconsistent`, a reviewer that answered and was incoherent, where this one is a provider that did not answer at all — and R-1008 registers the track-level failure to land a repair, which this instance adds to rather than restates. THE MEASUREMENT, read from `.data/jobs/86f628f5e4fb4e0c/job.json` and its run log rather than from the run's own summary: `execution_config` records `builder_source` and `reviewer_source` both `cli` and both models `claude-sonnet-4-6`, so the role resolution of DECISION amend0920-selfuse-real D2 worked and the run was NOT the raw `fake` fallback; the run lasted 513.84 seconds; `tasks[0].final_status` is `provider_unavailable` with `repair_rounds_allowed` 2 and neither spent; `result.diff` is 0 bytes, so no builder output reached the gate either, not merely no reviewer output; the job log holds three events of which the only non-routine one is `task_run_failed` with outcome `completion_gate_failed` and an empty `metadata`; and `worktree.cleanup_status` is `retained`, which is why a third `remedy/job-*` worktree survives the round. WHY MEDIUM AND NOT LOW: the `claude` binary IS on PATH and ran for both roles of this session, so "provider unavailable" here is not a missing dependency a reader can act on — the record names the outcome and nothing on disk names the CAUSE, and a self-use track that cannot say why its provider returned nothing cannot be repaired by reading its own evidence, which is the property the track exists to have. FIX: the `claude-cli` provider path records, on a `provider_unavailable` outcome, the invocation it attempted and the failure it observed — exit status, stderr, or the timeout it hit — into the job record beside `final_status`, so the next occurrence is diagnosable from the artifacts the closure already keeps. Not in F277's scope, which is the CLI's own machine contracts and not the provider layer. Owner: F282.

- R-1027 — Medium, THE CAUSE OF R-1016, MEASURED: IT IS NOT A NESTED-LAUNCH REFUSAL, AND THE REAL CAUSE IS STILL UNKNOWN BECAUSE THE FAILED RUN RECORDED NO EXIT CODE AND NO STDERR IN THE JOB RECORD. Registered by operator amendment amend0921-operator-feedback on 2026-09-21. The orchestrator's hypothesis was that the `claude-cli` provider launches the `claude` binary from inside a session that is itself Claude Code, that Claude Code refuses such a nested launch, and that the refusal goes to stderr where the provider drops it. THE MEASUREMENT: the exact child command the provider builds on its default path, `build_claude_cli_args(<claude>, "Reply with the single word OK.", write_mode="none", model="claude-sonnet-4-6")`, which is `claude -p "Reply with the single word OK." --output-format json --model claude-sonnet-4-6`, was run twice from this Claude Code session (CLI version 2.1.278) through the provider's own spawn point `pingpong_provider._guarded_cli_run`. Reading 1, with the inherited environment, which carries ten `CLAUDE*` variables including `CLAUDECODE` and `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION`: exit code 0, 6.5 seconds, stdout 1769 bytes (a valid JSON result envelope), and a one-line stderr whose last twenty lines are the single line `Warning: no stdin data received in 3s, proceeding without it. If piping from a slow command, redirect stdin explicitly: < /dev/null to skip, or wait longer.` Reading 2, with every variable whose name starts with `CLAUDE` removed by `env -u`: exit code 0, 2.3 seconds, stdout 1769 bytes, and an empty stderr. A nested launch therefore works in both environments, the hypothesis is refuted, and the second fix the amendment offered (stripping the `CLAUDE*` variables from the child's environment) was NOT applied, because it would fix nothing that was measured. The one difference between the readings is the three-second stdin wait, which costs seconds and not the 514 seconds R-1016 recorded; the original run's own job record under the data root could not be read from this session, so its error text was not re-read. FIX: this amendment made the cause visible for the next occurrence. The `claude-cli` provider now raises a typed exit error carrying the child's exit code and the last twenty lines of its stderr, passed through `stream_evidence.redact_text`; both `BuilderOutput` and `ReviewerOutput` carry them as `exit_code` and `stderr_tail`; the run record's builder and reviewer blocks carry both keys whenever the child exited nonzero; and a job task that ends `provider_unavailable` records the run's error text in a new `final_status_detail` field of `job.json`. The change is commit `f296ce90`, and its tests are in `tests/orchestration/test_claude_cli_failure_detail.py`, red-proved by four mutations. What remains is the cause itself: the next self-use run that ends `provider_unavailable` names its exit code and stderr on disk, or names a timeout in its error text, and whoever reads it registers the cause. Owner: F282.

- R-1029 — Low, SESSION BOOTSTRAP READS ALL OF decisions.md. Registered by operator amendment amend0921-operator-feedback on 2026-09-21, measured at `origin/main` `d0d40e89` plus this amendment's commits, after searching the live ledger and its archive for a finding that names `decisions.md` together with bootstrap, cold start or context cost, and finding none. THE SIZES: `.agent/decisions.md` is 1,799,220 bytes, `.agent/live_review.md` is 433,373 bytes, `.agent/live_review_archive.md` is 4,432,550 bytes, and `.agent/authored/` holds 32,423,769 bytes. THE RULE AS WRITTEN: neither `docs/agents/self_drive_protocol.md` nor `docs/agents/planner_reviewer_prompt.md` contains a sentence that orders a session to read `.agent/decisions.md` in full at start, and AGENTS.md's Session Resume list does not name the file. The only sentence that treats it as part of every session's start is the rationale in the self-drive protocol's sessions paragraph: "each session boundary re-buys the full cold start — protocol, handoff, ledger, decisions". The cost is therefore a habit the protocol describes rather than a rule it states, and at 1.8 megabytes the file no longer fits a session's context whole, so a session that follows the habit either spends most of its context on it or reads it partially without saying which part. FIX: bootstrap reads the last N decisions and the decisions the open feature file names. The protocol is not changed by this amendment. Owner: F282.

Gate: F283 R2 — the F283 round 2 entry, T001 SLICE B PART ONE. VERDICT PASS ON ALL SIX GATES. THE THREADING WAS PROVED BY CALLING THE HANDLER, not by reading the diff: `_cmd_job_budget_set(job_id="zzzznotajob", field_name="not_a_field", raw_value="5", json_output=True)` exits 2 with an EMPTY stderr and a stdout envelope carrying `unknown_budget_field`, `ok` false and `schema_version` 1, and the same call with the flag False exits 2 with an EMPTY stdout and the prose line on stderr. THE WORKER REPORTED A MUTATION THAT DID NOT GO RED, which is what gate G5(b) asked of it and what a weaker handback would have hidden: renaming the token at the `_refuse_budget_set` call site leaves the suite green, because no test names that token yet. DECISION F283 D1 (2026-09-21, round 3) — R-1020's REGISTRATION IS CORRECTED ON THE RECORD RATHER THAN RE-REGISTERED. This is a DECISION record and not a second `- R-1020 — ` block on purpose: `scripts/rotate_live_review.py::select_movable` moves a finding pair only when its id has EXACTLY ONE registration record and EXACTLY ONE `Done:` record, so a second registration line would jam R-1020's pair in the live ledger for good. Reverse this correction by deleting this paragraph; the registration above stays byte-identical either way, because the record is append-only. THE CORRECTION, appended by the planner and reviewer of F283's first session at the round 3 authoring dry run, because two load-bearing numbers in that paragraph landed FALSE and the record is append-only. The registration named "three shared EXITING helpers — `resolve_job_id`, `_exit_ambiguous` and `require_job_plan`" and counted "52 of the catalog's 111 `supports_json` commands". BOTH ARE WRONG, and the error is the reviewer's own: `require_job_plan` does not exit. It is in `packages/orchestration/pingpong_job.py`, its body contains no `sys.exit` and two `raise` statements, and it raises `JobNotFoundError` or `JobStoreError` — which is precisely what the handlers F283 rounds 1 and 2 migrated already catch. `lookup_job_id` likewise raises. Counting it as an exiting helper inflated both the helper list and the command count. RE-MEASURED at `fc8dfd85` by reading each helper's body for a `sys.exit` call and then counting call sites: the EXITING helpers are TWO, `resolve_job_id` and `_exit_ambiguous`, both in `packages/orchestration/data_paths.py`; they are called from NINE modules under `apps/cli/` at TWENTY-THREE call sites — `change` 3, `contract_cmd` 1, `decision` 1, `job` 6, `job_context_cmd` 1, `job_stop_cmd` 1, `patch` 7, `project` 1, `teacher_cmd` 2 — and TWENTY-EIGHT of the catalog's 111 `supports_json` commands live in one of those nine. The finding's SUBSTANCE is unchanged and was never in doubt: `job show --json` on a bad id really did write prose to stderr and leave stdout empty with the flag threaded, the strict xfail really did report XFAIL, and 28 of 111 is still a quarter of the JSON surface, so the ruling that this repair comes BEFORE T001's catalog half STANDS. Only the magnitude was overstated, by a factor of about two, and by a helper that belonged on the other side of the line. WHAT ROUND 3 DOES ABOUT IT, and why this finding stays OPEN afterwards: round 3 adds `apps/cli/job_id_arg.py::resolve_job_id_or_fail`, which catches what `lookup_job_id` raises and refuses through `fail()` — a LAYER above the resolver rather than a change to it, because the envelope is a CLI contract and pushing it down into `packages/` would make the storage layer depend on the shape of a command's stdout. It moves `job.py`'s SIX call sites onto it and turns the xfail green, deleting the mark in the same commit. That leaves SEVENTEEN call sites in the eight other modules, so R-1020 is repaired for one module and OPEN for the rest; a second guard in `tests/cli/test_job_refusal_envelope.py` counts those seventeen and reds if the number rises. R-1020 REMAINS OPEN with its owner unchanged: F283 — Machine contracts, part two.

- R-1034 — Low, `ui stop` IS CLASSIFIED `read_only` IN THE CATALOG THOUGH IT STOPS EVERY RUNNING UI SESSION. Registered by the planner and reviewer of F283's fifth session at the round 20 gate, measured at `a100a48a` while choosing which commands the round 21 sweep may execute, after searching the open set under checklist item 30 for `ui stop`, `action_class`, `read_only` and `catalog` and finding no open finding that describes it. The catalog entry `ui.stop` declares `action_class="read_only"` with `may_mutate_repo` and `may_execute_commands` both false, and its handler stops the local UI servers and reports what it stopped; a read-only classification is what every sweep, every permission rule and every reader of the catalog uses to decide what is safe to run unattended, and this command changes local state. The neighbouring classes exist: `data reclaim`, which deletes files under the data root, declares `local_state_change`. Nothing on disk is wrong today because no caller acts on the class for this command, which is why this is Low and not higher, and the round 21 sweep therefore excludes `ui.stop` BY NAME with this finding as its reason rather than trusting the class. Fix: the entry declares the class its handler earns, and any test that derives a read-only set from the catalog is re-measured in the same commit — including `TestReadOnlyWithoutSupportsJSONRatchet` in `tests/cli/test_json_envelope.py`, whose rule (DECISION F277 D7) is `not may_mutate_repo and not may_execute_commands` and which is therefore unaffected by the class name alone. Owner: the next findings-paydown feature, which `docs/roadmap/STATUS.md` names as F282 at `a100a48a`.
Landed: R-1033 — apps/cli/commands/stats_ledger_cmd.py's _cmd_stats_report now answers --json success via emit_ok(**cost_report_json(...)), keeping every key the old bare document carried, with tests/cli/test_stats_report.py's readers updated in the same commit; landed at this round's C4 (d63c48ff).

- R-1035 — Medium, THE CLOSURE'S SELF-USE RUN ENDED `provider_unavailable` WITH NO REVIEWER OUTPUT, AND THE RECORDED STATE CANNOT TELL A MISSING PROVIDER FROM A TIMED-OUT ONE. Registered by the planner and reviewer of F283's fifth session at the round 22 gate, as closure precondition 6 requires of every string `packages.orchestration.self_use_findings.describe_self_use_run_defects` returns, after searching the open set under checklist item 30 for `provider_unavailable`, `self_use`, `reviewer output` and `claude-cli` and finding no open finding that describes it. ONE id for both strings, because they are one defect reported at two levels: `.agent/selfuse_f283/run_defects.txt`, committed at round 22's `2087067b`, reads `job 129b3ad7206d4f8d (blocked): task_T001_gate_failed: final_status=provider_unavailable; missing_reviewer_output` and `T001 (blocked): completion_gate_failed: final_status=provider_unavailable; missing_reviewer_output`. What was measured: the run resolved both roles from the one `self_use` role configuration exactly as DECISION amend0920-selfuse-real D2 asks — `.agent/selfuse_f283/execution_config.txt` records builder and reviewer `claude-cli` at model `claude-sonnet-4-6`, effort `medium`, source `cli`, never a fake or local fallback — ran for 513 seconds by `.agent/selfuse_f283/timing.txt`, and ended with the job `blocked` and task T001 carrying no reviewer verdict at all. The run reached the provider seam and came back empty, and that is the whole of what the record shows: the job's `timeout_sec` is 120 while the run itself lasted 513 seconds, so a reviewer call cut off by that timeout and a provider that was genuinely unreachable produce the SAME recorded state, which is why this is Medium rather than Low — a self-use run that cannot distinguish "the provider was not there" from "we did not wait long enough" cannot be acted on without re-running it by hand. Nothing on the branch is wrong because of it: the item was planned, run to the approval gate and never applied, which is what the precondition asks, and `scripts/self_use_queue.json`'s entry SU-026 stays pending for a later close. Fix: the runner's own recorded state distinguishes the two cases — a provider that answered nothing within the call timeout is recorded as a timeout with the elapsed seconds and the limit it hit, and a provider that could not be invoked at all keeps `provider_unavailable` — and whichever of the two this run was is then readable from the record rather than inferred from a wall clock. Owner: the next findings-paydown feature, which `docs/roadmap/STATUS.md` names as F282 at `15145f71`.
